# Project Priority Map

> **Created**: 2026-04-25
> **Purpose**: Single resolver for "what should we work on next?". Consolidates the various roadmap documents (service architecture, agent refactor, SA roadmap, signal detection, RL, rename) into one ordered backlog.
> **How to use**: Before starting a new feature, scope shift, multi-slice line, or branch, check this map. If the work isn't here, either add it (with justification) or argue the active P0/P1 should change. New multi-slice lines must pass this check before their first implementation slice.
> **When to update**: Whenever a P0/P1 item completes, blocks, or a higher-priority item emerges. Insert at the TOP of §10 Decision Log (newest-first ordering — see the note inside that section).
> **Small evidenced debt**: `docs/design/ENGINEERING_ISSUE_REGISTER.md` is the single owner for bounded issues that do not yet require a dedicated workstream. Contract violations, design decisions, protected-boundary changes, and active slices stay in this map rather than being hidden in the register.

## 1. TL;DR

> **Project identity (Phase 2 rename EXECUTED 2026-05-31)**: Local repo directory + code/docs references renamed `MindfulRL-Intraday` → `ArkScope`. The only remaining `mindfulrl` (lowercase) references are intentional and must NOT be "fixed": Native Messaging host id `com.mindfulrl.sa_alpha_picks`, Firefox addon id `sa-alpha-picks@mindfulrl.local`, and historical/archived docs. A further *product-brand* rename may STILL happen if the local-first workbench positioning settles on a different name (AIRA Desk / ARK Desk are draft-only) — until then do NOT preemptively introduce a fourth name, and do NOT block development on rename hygiene. Execution detail: git history + memory `project_rename_arkscope.md` (the executed runbook `ARKSCOPE_RENAME_PHASE2.md` was removed 2026-06-07, recoverable via git).

> **Formal product repositioning 2026-05-02**: This is no longer an "intraday RL trading research repo" or "agent/tool backend for analysis". The project transitions to **local-first financial research agent workbench** — a single-user research environment that combines (a) Hermes-like agent capabilities (persistent memory, skills, tools, scheduler, replay, compression, subagents), (b) this codebase's existing financial data layer (news / SA / macro / prices / fundamentals / signals), and (c) a research GUI that lets the user read, edit, schedule, and migrate research artefacts. **North star**: the agent reads its own accumulated knowledge substrate across sessions and machines; the user sees and edits everything via a research GUI; zipping the profile directory moves a researcher's work between machines. **Five-layer architecture**: Workbench UI → Agent Layer → Data Layer → Profile Layer → Portability Layer (top-to-bottom). **Hermes is capability reference, NOT product reference** — we borrow filesystem-first profile + daemon scheduler + FTS5 cross-session recall + skills/tools flexibility, but our differentiation is the financial data layer + investment research workflow, not general-purpose agent platform. **Phase C PAUSED** — runner refactor is now second-order to product boundary, profile, sync, GUI IA, and agent-data-access API. Active sequence: audit done → spec done (`b0743da`) → **docs governance cleanup** (audit `docs/design/DOCS_GOVERNANCE_AUDIT_2026_05.md` + follow-up archive/delete/rewrite commit) → read-only UI skeleton against existing reads → **two-SQLite split + transient DuckDB** migration first cut (kill criterion: zip-and-go on a second machine). Phase C resumes only after `workbench v1 ship + 2 wks stable + 1 verified migration`. Repo identity renamed to `ArkScope` (Phase 2 / P3.2 executed 2026-05-31; only intentional `mindfulrl` lowercase for host/addon remains). Detail in §10 newest entry.

### Standing premises for agent-capability work (ruled 2026-08-17)

Not re-litigated per slice:

1. Private untracked files are inventoried before deletion, and purposeless
   historical tails are not kept. The inventory is its own slice, bounded to
   this project's own files and named temporary trees — never a sweep of
   arbitrary `/tmp`.
2. Discord is only a replaceable alert transport. No core path may depend on a
   specific transport existing.
3. Agent capability is anchored on stable tool / API / MCP contracts; OpenClaw,
   Hermes, DeepSeek and similar harnesses are replaceable adapters. The
   question is always whether a harness can attach to our contracts, never
   whether we rewrite to its shape. Whether those contracts should converge is
   still undecided.
4. Alerts, bots, and unattended flows are redesigned, not restored. Lessons may
   be read from history; the retired code holds no product status.

Queued under these premises: private untracked inventory slice; private
`config/.env` `DISCORD_*` cleanup (pending authorization, key names only,
restart afterwards); model catalog / channel capability refresh (Anthropic
registry still lacks Opus 5; the UI must separate officially-known,
credential-visible, and task-tested; `GPT-5.3-Codex-Spark` stays a
Card-translation candidate pending a canary and a Traditional Chinese quality
comparison); Document Intelligence, whose `pdf-inspector` evaluation needs its
own grounding spike.

## 2. P0 — Immediate

### P0.1 Replay Harness (Major Refactor Phase 0)

- **Why**: Gates subsequent agent changes by detecting structural drift in
  tools, argument shape, prompt identity, nested subagent expectations, and
  compression metadata without making a live model call.
- **Current contract**: Anthropic and OpenAI programmatic captures share one
  explicit-entrypoint schema and one resolver
  (`ToolRegistry -> server_tools -> bridge_tools`). Required pins must
  resolve; unrelated registry additions remain valid.
- **Current fixtures**: six fixtures cover no-tool, one-tool, OpenAI parity,
  Layer 0 overflow, and nested subagent behavior. There is no current
  document-input replay branch; future Document Intelligence owns that design.
- **Status**: ✅ SHIPPED. The original 2026-04/05 implementation sequence is
  preserved in Git history. `docs/design/P0_1_FULL_V1_SPEC.md` is the
  refreshed current authority; `tests/test_replay_fixtures.py` is the
  provider-free regression gate.

### P0.2 Service-first S2: jobs persistence + history API

- **Why**: `/jobs/status` and `/jobs/run` exist but state is in-process. DB persistence is foundation for S3 dashboard, OpenClaw orchestration, and scheduler observability. Zero blast radius (no agent path changes).
- **Acceptance criteria**:
  - `job_runs` table (sql/0XX migration): job_id, name, started_at, finished_at, status (running/success/failed), last_error, payload (jsonb), trigger_source.
  - `job_definitions` table (or config-driven): name, schedule, last_success_at, last_error_at, next_run_at, enabled.
  - `/jobs/history?name=&limit=&offset=` paginated.
  - `/jobs/status` reads from DB (`last_success` + `last_error` + `next_run` from latest rows per job).
  - Existing job runners updated to write a row at start and update at finish (transactional so partial failures don't leak).
  - 5 sample queries pass: "last error for SA refresh", "jobs that haven't run in 24h", "all failed jobs in last 7d", etc.
- **Effort**: 2-3 days incl. migration.
- **Status**: ✅ done 2026-04-25 (commit `a86c2ab`). Delivered: `sql/011_add_job_runs.sql` (status enum running/succeeded/failed, trigger_source free-text, payload+result jsonb, message+error split, duration_ms, two indexes for name + status time-series queries); `src/service/job_runs_store.py::JobRunsStore` with `create_run`/`finish_run`/`list_runs` (paginated, clamped) / `latest_runs_by_name` — all best-effort so DB unavailability degrades to process-local state; `src/service/jobs.py::run_job` records start (running) and finish (succeeded/failed) rows with `trigger_source` threaded through; `list_jobs_status` merges DB-latest with process-local fallback; `GET /jobs/history?name=&limit=&offset=` with pydantic `JobRunRow` / `JobsHistoryResponse` models in `src/api/routes/jobs.py`. 27 tests covering store CRUD, availability gating, error swallowing, pagination clamping, DB-merge + fallback, persistence on success/failure, and endpoint round-trip. Smoke-tested against the live DB. Per priority-map decision, v1 keeps `job_definitions` in-code (static catalog merged with DB latest at read time) — separate `job_definitions` table deferred until a config-driven schedule actually shows up as a need. Unblocks P1.5 S3 admin dashboard.

### P0.3 SA Comment Intelligence Stage 1 (rule-based) ✅ COMPLETED 2026-04-25

- **Why**: 21K+ comments already in `sa_article_comments`. Rule-based extraction is near-zero cost and immediately useful for ticker-mention discovery and high-value-comment surfacing. **No LLM in Stage 1** — keep it deterministic and cheap.
- **Delivered** (commits `aa76cb0` Stage 1, `5e72f6f` v1.1 fixes, `ba66fde` version filter pin):
  - `sa_comment_signals` table (sql/012) with `comment_row_id BIGINT FK`, denorm article_id+comment_id, `ticker_mentions[]` + `candidate_mentions[]`, `keyword_buckets jsonb` (matched terms preserved), `high_value_score numeric`, `needs_verification bool`, `rule_set_version text`. GIN on tickers + score DESC + extracted_at + article_id indexes.
  - Detection rules in `src/sa/comment_signals.py`; documented in `docs/design/SA_COMMENT_INTELLIGENCE_PLAN.md` §5.1. Rule-set version `v1.1`.
  - Backfill via `extract_sa_comment_signals` job (`src/sa/comment_signal_backfill.py`), gated on `sa_enabled`, observable through S2 `job_runs`.
  - Tool: `list_high_value_comments(window_days, ticker, min_score, limit)` registered in registry + Anthropic + OpenAI bridges. Pinned to current `RULE_SET_VERSION` so partial re-extracts cannot leak stale signals.
  - 38 unit/integration tests pass; 20-row spot-check on score≥4 gave **~85% precision** on `ticker_mentions` (target ≥80% met).
  - Production backfill: 36,352 comments in ~95 seconds, universe = 102 tickers, 1,508 score≥5 / 481 score≥8 / 10,267 with ticker_mentions / 11,358 with candidate_mentions / 3,508 needs_verification / 189 dot-suffix tickers found.
- **Deferred to Stage 2**: `get_sa_comment_consensus` (needs sentiment direction to mean bullish/bearish), thread/parent grouping, LLM summarization on Stage 1 high-score candidates.

### P0.4 SA extension reliability & Market News recovery

- **Why**: Daily-use feed. Already exists but has freshness gaps, intermittent anti-bot issues, and incomplete article bodies. Smaller patches that interleave with P0.1-P0.3 — **not** a new main thrust.
- **Delivered (observability slice)** 2026-04-25:
  - Three-layer health report (`freshness` / `feed_health` / `detail_health`) with `ok` / `warning` / `critical` severity; thresholds visible in response. `src/service/sa_market_news_health.py` + `GET /sa/market-news/health`. Strict mode upgrades any non-ok severity to HTTP 503. Market-hours rule: zero published items in 24h is critical only during regular US session, warning otherwise. (commit `d923177`)
  - Chrome extension SA syncs (alpha-picks refresh, market-news, articles) record into `job_runs` via new `record_extension_job` native_host action + `JobRunsStore.record_completed_run()`. Slug `sa_extension:<flow>` keeps these distinct from in-process jobs but visible to `/jobs/status` and `/jobs/history`. (commit `78e56d2`)
- **Reliability incident and reviewed response** 2026-07-19 through 2026-07-25:
  - Firefox packaging omitted `article_identity.js`; manual copy/reload restored capture, and the shipped dependency-closure builder now makes the omission a hard pre-install failure.
  - 25 Market News runs recorded `detail_failed=18` while outer `job_runs.status` remained `succeeded`, proving that the existing telemetry can hide degraded detail work.
  - The combined design is written at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-sa-extension-reliability-control-clarity-design.md`. It locks packaging closure first, status derived from structured phase/item truth, four-state audited repairs, 24h routine catch-up plus no-age recorded-ID retry and 168h-capped incident recovery, five grouped popup controls with honest bounds, and fresh approval of the historical repair manifest. The prior 27-item observation is not a constant; a 2026-07-25 read-only reconstruction found 118 unique failed IDs, 88 now body-present, and 30 still missing.
- **Still deferred**:
  - Auto-recovery on anti-bot rejection: delayed retry with backoff, alternate endpoint. Holding until health report shows chronic anti-bot failure mode worth automating against.
  - Generic arbitrary-range or lifetime detail backfill. The reviewed incident operation is bounded recovery, not evidence that SA exposes complete history.
  - `/sa/market-news` p95 latency <500ms monitoring (existing query has 3 indexes; add FastAPI middleware only if read latency starts mattering for downstream agents).
- **Status**: ✅ **LIVE COMPLETE 2026-07-26**. The SA extension reliability batch remains live through merged tip `2ef8bb69`: backend `+91/-2`, frontend `+8/-1`, popup contrast gate included, historical repair `88 already_present / 30 repaired / 0 unavailable / 0 retryable`, all `118` bodies present, and installed Chrome/Firefox readback green. Firefox remains the sole auto-sync owner by operating choice; Chrome is manual fallback, and the app-owned collector remains an unadopted P2.6 Part 2 decision. Calibration Anthropic-refusal normalization is live through `8e73dba4`. Coverage v2 is also **LIVE COMPLETE** through merged product tip `3f0fb18f`: backend `+76/-40` to `4749`, focused `230`; frontend `+12/-3` to `96/1072`, focused `118`; resources `714/1814/44`; scanner `36/20/0/20`; typecheck/build/local-runtime and production read-only blank-row evidence green. No provider, scheduler, repair, or production-write action occurred during Coverage closeout.

## 3. P1 — Next

### P1.1 Multi-factor signals → tool/API exposure ✅ COMPLETED 2026-04-26

- **Why**: `src/signals/` already computed factor signals (sector_momentum, event_chain, sentiment_anomaly, volume_spike). Exposing as a per-factor breakdown + cross-sectional rank converts existing latent value to active value, with explicit "recommendation, not prediction" framing. NOT a price-prediction claim.
- **Delivered** (commit `550c4da`):
  - `get_signal_factors(ticker, days, as_of_date, strategy)` tool — returns per-factor `{factor_type, impact, weight, contribution, details}` rows + a `data_quality` block (`news_count`, `scored_news_count`, `missing_factors`, `errors`) so callers can tell "no data" from "neutral signal". Refactored `synthesize_signal` to share `_compute_signal_context`; existing `TradingSignal` contract preserved.
  - `GET /signals/factor-rank?universe=&factor=&top=&days=&as_of_date=` — universe `watchlist` | `alpha_picks` (alpha_picks deduped). Factor enum maps URL-friendly names to synthesizer factor_type strings — `volume_spike` resolves to `VOLUME_SPIKE` (output factor_type), NOT `volume_anomaly` (synthesizer's input bucket). News df preloaded ONCE per request and shared across per-ticker calls. Single global anchor (caller-supplied or `df.max()`) so the cross-section is reproducible. `missing_data_tickers` carries reasons; no-data tickers never enter the ranked list. `notes[]` surfaces caveats — sector_momentum ranking flags that the factor is sector-shared, composite ranking flags that no validation has been wired yet.
  - 30 new unit/integration tests in `tests/test_signal_factors_p1.py` covering shape, factor mapping (`volume_spike → VOLUME_SPIKE`), universe dedup, news_df preloaded once (call-counter), single global anchor, missing-data routing, top-N truncation, invalid factor/universe → 422, sector caveat. Existing `test_synthesize_signal` contract test still green.
- **Deferred** (deliberately out of P1.1 scope):
  - Signal validation: forward-return decile analysis (top vs bottom 1d/5d/20d returns) sits on top of this exposure; not yet wired.
  - Entry / exit timing logic — that's a strategy/backtest layer, not a ranking exposure.
  - SA comment intelligence as a factor — Stage 1 has no sentiment direction, so it can only mean attention not bullish/bearish. Hold.
- **Status**: ✅ done 2026-04-26 (commit `550c4da`).

### P1.2 Free calendar / macro: FRED + Finnhub calendar

- **Why**: CPI / FOMC / GDP / earnings calendar / IPO calendar. Cheap, broadly useful in analysis & monitor flows. Strictly precedes paid macro subscriptions.
- **Acceptance criteria** (revised vs initial draft after `P1_2_PROVIDER_DISCOVERY.md` smoke results):
  - `data_sources/fred_client.py` — FRED series + ALFRED vintage replay (free API key, smoke §6).
  - `data_sources/finnhub_calendar_client.py` — `/calendar/economic`, `/calendar/earnings`, `/calendar/ipo` with UTC-aware parse (smoke §5.2).
  - `src/macro_calendar/store.py` — canonical/revision upsert with as-of read; `MacroCalendarStore`.
  - `src/macro_calendar/{fred,finnhub}_ingestion.py` — pure ingestion functions (no API/tools coupling).
  - 6 jobs in `/jobs/status` gated on `macro_calendar.enabled`: `fetch_fred_series`, `fetch_fred_release_dates`, `fetch_economic_calendar_recent`, `fetch_economic_calendar_backfill`, `fetch_earnings_calendar`, `fetch_ipo_calendar`.
  - Health/freshness telemetry — `compute_macro_calendar_health` + `/macro/health[?strict]` (jobs cadence + table coverage, market-hours upgrade for `recent`).
  - Read API + agent tool surface — final shape: 4 routes (`/macro/economic-calendar`, `/macro/earnings-calendar`, `/macro/ipo-calendar`, `/macro/series/{series_id}`) + 2 tools (`get_economic_calendar`, `get_macro_value`). All gate on `macro_calendar.enabled`, support `?as_of=` (date inputs are EOD UTC; `from_date` is SOD).
- **Effort**: 1 day → revised to ~5 days incremental over 6 commits (see `P1_2_SPEC.md` §9). Provider smoke + revision-log semantics turned out to be the load-bearing parts.
- **Status**: ✅ done 2026-04-27. All 6 commits + 2 follow-ups landed (`e8fc1db`/`926a81c` schema+DAL → `f03cf86`/`6536652`/`959c589` FRED → `d3ec85a`/`5b376aa`/`35e8d4f` Finnhub+rename → `2edea5e`/`3bde0ec`/`6cca46a` jobs+API → `b8454a2`/`a5b24d3` health → `2478e74` read API+tools → `7eeffb0` review findings → `95a644d` from_date/to_date boundary). Final at `95a644d`.

### P1.3 SA Digest / Reading Workflow v1

- **Why**: SA data already in our DB (Alpha Picks articles, Market News feed, Stage 1 comment signals) but the agent doesn't proactively surface it in decision moments — the bottleneck is **usage**, not **coverage**. Original framing was "SA Latest Articles metadata v1" (more data); reframed after review to "make existing SA data actually enter watchlist daily / earnings prep flows" via a deterministic evidence-pack tool + targeted skill prompt updates. See `P1_3_SPEC.md`.
- **Acceptance criteria** (see `P1_3_SPEC.md` §3, §8 for detail):
  - Tool `get_sa_digest(ticker, days=14, max_articles=5, max_news=5, max_comments=8, min_comment_score=4.0)` — pulls from `sa_articles` + `sa_market_news` + `sa_comment_signals` ↔ `sa_article_comments`; returns a deterministic structured evidence pack with `recent_articles[]`, `high_discussion_news[]`, `high_value_comments.{ticker_mentions[], candidate_mentions[]}`, `data_quality`, `source_notes[]`.
  - Selection rules locked: comments reuse Stage 1 `high_value_score`, ≤3 per article (anti-domination), `needs_verification=True` rows kept (not filtered), ticker vs candidate mention split preserved.
  - **No internal LLM call** in v1 — evidence pack is deterministic; main agent produces Chinese summary in its own context. v1.1 candidate: `summary_mode="llm"` opt-in.
  - **No new SA data sources** — v1 is read-only over existing tables. New scrapers, transcripts, StockTalks, author tracking all deferred.
  - Skill prompt updates: `earnings_prep` (must call, days=30) and `full_analysis` (recommended, days=14). Skills are prompt templates, not executable workflows — the acceptance test asserts the body contains the canonical instruction string + tool name.
  - 18 acceptance tests covering disabled-state, window clamps, ordering + caps, mention split, per-article cap, score cutoff, `needs_verification` passthrough, data_quality always-present, source_notes disclaimer, per-source failure isolation, ticker uppercasing, JSON serializability, excerpt truncation, registry + bridge wiring, skill body content (×2).
- **Out of scope (v1.1+ candidates)**: earnings-call transcripts (on-demand spike first), author-tracking pipeline (needs curated author list), event-gated market-news comment threads (extension change required), `portfolio_scan` skill integration (defer until SA-coverage rate observed), market-wide digest, server-side scrapers.
- **Effort**: 1-2 days, 3 commits (tool + tests → registry + bridges → skill prompts + docs).
- **Status**: ✅ done 2026-04-27. Spec at `docs/design/P1_3_SPEC.md`. Commit chain: `ce5f780` spec → `1108310` 5 review findings → `55c02bd` tool + 18 unit tests → `70065c5` 4 commit-1 follow-ups (clamp, SQL canary, spec sync) → `a19441e` registry + bridges + 12-file count sync → `68a60a5` dispatch regression test + 3 stale docstrings → `fd9fb80` envelope assertion → `1db2789` skill prompts + acceptance tests + this status flip.

### P1.4 Context Compression Phase B

- **Why**: Long agent runs and large tool results will eventually hit the context limit. **Framing**: P1.4 is not a defensive cap — it's what enables more tool calls and (later) multi-round runs to be safe under sustained load. Large tool outputs become summaries in-prompt + raw on disk (retrievable); compaction becomes a normal operating layer rather than a panic mode.
- **Acceptance criteria**: see `docs/design/P1_4_SPEC.md` for the locked-in shape. Spec encodes 5 guarantees (compressor is a library not a runner; subagent path unchanged; Layer 0 net-positive for heavy queries; no fabricated reasoning; per-layer toggles), the 7-layer design (Layers 0-3 deterministic + Layer 4 provider-native optional + Layer 5 LLM full compact + Layer 6 anchor recovery), per-tool reducer policy, and 5-commit decomposition.
- **Borrowed patterns** from `~/PycharmProjects/AI_Agent_Researcher` (see P1.4 spec §2): marker-based summary, recent-boundary algorithm, circuit breaker (3 consecutive failures → off). NOT borrowed: drop-in `ClientCompactionSession` (OpenAI-specific; we're dual-provider) or SDK Session injection (proven painful).
- **Effort**: 4-5 days, 5 commits (overflow_store → deterministic layers → Anthropic integration → observability → LLM full compact).
- **Status**: ✅ SHIPPED. `docs/design/P1_4_SPEC.md` is the refreshed
  current authority. Context compression remains a library with durable
  overflow storage, deterministic Layers 0-3, optional provider-native Layer
  4, threshold-driven Layer 5, Layer 6 anchors, and a three-failure circuit
  breaker. Both the master client-side path and Layer 5 default off; there is
  no current presentation command or one-turn activation path. The original
  five-commit implementation sequence remains in Git history.

### P1.5 S3 admin dashboard — OSS-first survey

- **Why**: Operational visibility (jobs, freshness, SA sync, reports, errors). **Survey OSS before building.** A custom dashboard would consume 1-2 weeks for a thin domain layer; an off-the-shelf admin we extend is better leverage.
- **Acceptance criteria**:
  - 1-day spike: stand up 2-3 OSS candidates against our schema. Candidates to evaluate:
    - **FastAPI Admin** / **sqladmin** (auto-CRUD over SQLAlchemy)
    - **RQ Dashboard** or **Prefect UI** (if jobs migrate to a queue)
    - **Apache Superset** (if analytical views are the priority, not ops)
    - **Custom minimal Next.js** (only if all above fail spec)
  - Decision doc: chosen tool, reasoning, gaps to fill, integration plan.
  - If chosen tool fits: 1-2 days integration. If "build custom": ~4 days minimal jobs/freshness/SA-sync/reports/errors view.
- **Effort**: 1d spike + 2-4d integration.
- **Status**: ✅ SPIKE DONE 2026-05-02. Decision doc at `docs/design/P1_5_S3_OSS_SPIKE_DECISION.md`. Outcome: sqladmin / FastAPI Admin / Superset / RQ-Prefect UI all rejected (SQLAlchemy gap + wrong shape — CRUD/BI vs ops-monitoring). Custom minimal (FastAPI + Jinja2 under `src/api/admin/`, ~300-500 LoC) recommended; passes all four non-negotiables (no ORM, single process, reuses existing JSON read paths, read-only default). HTMX additive in v1.1. Integration is NOT next: P1.5 stops here, Phase C spec begins immediately per the priority map's 2026-05-02 sequence decision. When integration cycle opens later, this doc is the input.

  1. **Code-reviewed capability registry** — tools/structured-output support, effort tiers, context/output limits, executable driver per model family. These are engineering contracts, not user data; they stay in reviewed code.

  3. **Effective picker** — intersection: visible to the current active credential AND executable for the task at hand. **Adopted default (Sol Q → yes): picker shows only verified-usable models; legacy/pinned/custom-ID/unverified-seed entries live in an Advanced section with explicit warnings.**
  4. Research/query/Card execution, routing, and Settings consume reviewed
     capability/effective views rather than a presentation-only catalog.
- **Current drift boundary**: provider facts belong in
  `src/model_capabilities.py`; credential visibility belongs in discovery
  cache; task eligibility belongs in effective views. Context, output,
  thinking, effort, routing, and prompt defaults must remain derived from or
  tested against those reviewed facts. The retired terminal view is not a
  compatibility authority.
- **Source material for slice-time verification (do not trust cached values)**: https://www.anthropic.com/claude/fable · https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models · https://help.openai.com/en/articles/20001354 (gpt-5.6 availability; actual visibility varies by account/plan/auth channel — which is exactly why discovery is per-credential). openai package already bumped to 2.45.0 on 2026-07-10 (stale-process ImportError incident recorded in the calibration closeout entry).

### P2.9 AI execution usage observability
- **Status**: design adopted 2026-07-12; implementation deliberately deferred and must not interrupt unfinished core product work. Authority: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-12-ai-execution-usage-observability-design.md`.
- **Why**: ArkScope already tracks tokens for agent loops and persists AI Research usage, but card synthesis/translation and other model tasks discard available duration/usage metadata. There is no complete per-user-action call tree or daily aggregate authority.
- **Adopted shape**: metadata-only `execution -> child execution -> provider call event` ledger in `profile_state.db`; every call links to one root user/system action and carries code-reviewed `feature / operation / trigger` classifications. Store nullable input/output/cache/reasoning tokens, wall/provider duration, provider/model/effort/auth and terminal state; never copy prompts or answers. Later translation/retry/regeneration is a new execution linked to the source artifact/execution.
- **Product surface**: future `System -> AI Usage` period dashboard plus contextual summaries in Research/Card. Settings is reserved for separately approved controls. V1 has no pricing, dollar cost, warnings, budgets, hard limits, backfill, or retention UI.
- **Future decomposition**: (1) all-seam capture foundation and coverage/health contract, (2) aggregation API and contextual summaries, (3) System dashboard. Do not open an implementation plan until this map explicitly promotes slice 1.

All review, exact canonical A/B, responsive/privacy/local-runtime, copied-profile, and required real-Gateway gates are closed.** Authorities: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-13-portfolio-1-1-observation-activity-design.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-13-portfolio-1-1-slice-1-capture-foundation.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-14-portfolio-1-1-slice-2-account-overview.md`, and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-15-portfolio-1-1-slice-3-activity-journal.md`.
- **Why now**: Holdings V1 persists canonical positions, but the existing IBKR call already discards account-value tags and broker realized P&L, while no execution/commission ledger exists. IB Gateway execution history is current-day bounded, so every uncaptured day creates a permanent activity gap.
- **Adopted architecture**: retain `portfolio_accounts` / `portfolio_positions` / `portfolio_position_notes` as current-state authority; add a non-cascading append-only observation layer in `profile_state.db` for capture runs, account snapshots, complete broker-position observations, immutable executions, commission revisions, corrections, unmatched changes, and atomic manual-adjustment journals. Observations never rebuild canonical holdings. `ibkr_review` derives preview at read time; `ibkr_auto` may apply only a complete position leg after observation commit.
- **Capture**: one read-only Gateway capture service (`portfolio_capture=+70`, distinct from `holdings=+60`), startup catch-up, configurable 5-1440 minute cadence with 15-minute default, and manual trigger. Account/execution/position legs report truthful five-state terminal outcomes; coverage gaps are explicit and never mean zero activity.
- **Product surface**: Holdings-first four-tab layout (`持倉` / `活動` / `帳戶明細` / `同步紀錄`), per-account IBKR NLV/cash/margin/P&L, separate manual-account subtotal, broker/canonical dual timestamps, correction-aware order/fill activity, user-confirmed intent, and no fake overall net worth. Data Sources does not duplicate the scheduler.
- **Sequence**: Slice 1 observation/capture foundation and minimal controls -> Slice 2 account overview -> Slice 3 activity/journal UI. Each slice gets its own plan, review, canonical A/B, and live Gateway gate. Flex history, performance return calculations, agent activity tools, multiple Gateways, and order placement are deferred. Future trading is expected but must use a separately authorized client-id domain, order ledger, confirmation, and kill switch; capture stays read-only.

## 6. Cross-Cutting: open-source-first principle

Where the option is "build it" vs "use OSS", default to a **time-boxed OSS spike (≤1 day) before committing to custom**. Applies to:

- S3 admin dashboard (P1.5)
- Future job queue if S2 outgrows in-process scheduler (RQ / Dramatiq / Prefect)
- Knowledge graph backend if NetworkX outgrows in-memory (Neo4j Community vs Memgraph vs Kùzu)
- Future analytics views (Superset / Metabase vs custom)

The spike should produce a decision doc, not just opinion. If OSS doesn't fit, document **what specifically failed** so future spikes don't repeat.

---

## 7. Subscription Strategy

Two **separate** decision frames. Don't mix.

### 7.1 Operational subscriptions (must-keep)

Question: "Would breaking this disrupt our current product workflow?"

| Service | Status | Why |
|---------|--------|-----|
| IBKR market data | active | quotes / options / price core; cannot be replaced |
| SA Alpha Picks | active | proven research workflow, extension already integrated |

### 7.2 Research subscriptions (evidence-gated)

Question: "Is the signal hypothesis pre-validated, with OOS plan and kill criteria?"

This is the bucket that ties to *signal-research investment plans* — distinct from operational must-keeps. Each candidate requires:

1. Written hypothesis (what edge / what regime / what universe).
2. OOS validation plan (windows, baseline, metric thresholds).
3. Kill criteria (when to cancel if hypothesis fails).
4. Cost vs value estimate.

| Candidate | When to consider | Notes |
|-----------|------------------|-------|
| Polygon Premium | only if real-time / minute-bar gap blocks intraday work | duplicate IBKR for many uses |
| Unusual Whales | only if options/event-driven monitor is built and validates options-flow signal | $50/mo sample; needs use case |
| Benzinga | only if news latency / analyst-rating gap is empirically proven | overlaps IBKR/Finnhub/SA |
| Financial Datasets API | already integrated as fallback; keep on-demand toggle | not pure subscription, pay-as-go cache |
| Provider-neutral hosted search | only after a task-specific evidence gap and provider/auth/harness capability are proven | optional agent-side context; Tavily retired 2026-08-24 and is not a signal source |

### 7.3 Not approved near-term

Reddit / X / StockTwits paid access — defer until a specific signal hypothesis is committed to writing.

## 9. Sequencing Recommendation

**Status (2026-05-02, post-pivot)**: P0.1-P0.4 + P1.1-P1.5 (spike) + Phase C spec all landed. **Phase C is paused**; product re-positioned to **local-first investment research workbench**. **Active sequence**: (1) `docs/design/LOCAL_FIRST_RESEARCH_WORKBENCH_AUDIT.md` — done 2026-05-02; six sections (north star + 5-layer architecture + 5 axes with sync surface as main axis + Hermes-capability gap); (2) `docs/design/LOCAL_FIRST_RESEARCH_WORKBENCH_SPEC.md` — locks product positioning, deployment model, storage strategy (**two-SQLite split (`workbench.db` app state + `sa_cache.db` SA ingest, isolated writer locks) + transient DuckDB on parquet** — no persistent `warehouse.duckdb` in v1; legacy reads were transitional), sync policy (v1 manual zip-and-go, v2 selective sync), page IA, **bidirectional DTO inventory (read + write actions, even if writes not yet implemented)**, **scheduler storage interface + process interface + deferral trigger** (NOT model A/B/C choice — that stays deferred per audit §7.4), Phase C resume gate, "first SQLite/DuckDB migration cut" kill criterion, and explicit decisions for all seven open questions (no spec-internal opens); (3) **docs governance cleanup** — audit at `docs/design/DOCS_GOVERNANCE_AUDIT_2026_05.md` (this commit) + follow-up archive/delete/rewrite commit, BEFORE code work begins (removes pre-pivot entrypoint pollution that would otherwise pull cut #1 back toward retired architecture or RL/CLI-first); (4) read-only UI skeleton against existing reads — validates product shape without committing to write paths; (5) two-SQLite migration first cut: `research_reports` first (small + already-files-on-disk) + zip-and-go smoke; (6) cross-machine smoke insertion-point: **one table migrated → immediate zip-and-go test on second machine** (do NOT defer to end). Phase C resumes only after the three-part gate (workbench ship + 2 weeks stable + 1 verified migration). P0.4 deferred items stay deferred; P2.2 Knowledge Graph stays deferred; P2.4 Key Markets opportunistic-only. Operational subscriptions (IBKR / SA Alpha Picks) unchanged; research subscriptions still evidence-gated.

**Locked decisions for this pivot (2026-05-02)**:
- **DuckDB scope**: added but strictly OLAP/columnar — news / price / macro / backtest / cross-source exploration. NOT for scheduler state, UI state, memory, settings, watchlist (those go to SQLite). DuckDB can `ATTACH` SQLite for cross-engine joins.
- **Packaging**: deferred. Spec only locks deployment target (local web app or desktop wrapper must read same local profile directory); PyInstaller / launcher script / Tauri choice waits until scheduler daemon model + storage profile are fixed. Premature packaging choice would prematurely bind process model.
- **Audit-now**: not optional — without an audit, the spec is a wishlist. Five axes, ~60-90 min, sync surface is the main axis (not an appendix) per "跨機同步是根本問題" framing.

The historical week plan below is kept for context.

Bonus effect: the same fix un-breaks sidecar `get_current_quote`'s IBKR path (previously silently fell back to local bars in worker threads). **Fragility note (recorded, not actionable now)**: `import ib_insync` itself needs an event loop at import time (eventkit module-level `get_event_loop()`) — every process today first-imports it on the main thread (the sidecar via eager route import at startup); if that import ever goes lazy into a worker thread, the import-time path re-opens and `_ensure_event_loop` cannot save it. Remaining for LIVE COMPLETE: restart sidecar → full Holdings UI verification (preview should now list the real account/positions) → flip plan header.
- **2026-07-09 (IBKR AnyIO worker-loop hotfix IMPLEMENTED FOR REVIEW — live read probe green)**: The first merged Holdings preview exposed a shared `IBKRDataSource` runtime defect: FastAPI sync handlers run in AnyIO worker threads, while `ib_insync.IB.connect()` internally calls `connectAsync` through the current asyncio loop; Python provides no loop in those threads, so connection failed before `managedAccounts`/`positions`/`portfolio`/`accountSummary` and emitted an unawaited-coroutine warning. Branch `codex/ibkr-worker-loop-fix` adds an owned temporary event loop only when the calling thread lacks a usable loop, releases it on disconnect or connection failure, and pins the lifecycle with a real worker-thread test. Focused IBKR/holdings/quote suites: 46 passed + 8 intentional skips. A direct read-only probe using the existing S-J provider config and `holdings=+60` client id reached the real Gateway and returned 1 visible account / 9 positions; no DB write and no order API. This shared fix also prevents current-quote and other synchronous IBKR consumers from hitting the same hidden worker-thread failure. Pending: review/merge, sidecar restart, then Holdings preview→apply→note-preservation UI verification before marking the parent slice LIVE COMPLETE.

Two tightenings vs the 2026-05-02 entry below: (1) v1 storage = **two-SQLite split (`workbench.db` app state + `sa_cache.db` SA ingest, independent per-file writer locks)** + **transient DuckDB on parquet** (NO persistent `warehouse.duckdb` in v1; `warehouse.duckdb` named as v2 candidate per spec §11.1); (2) **external ingest clients contract (LOCK #9)** — SA native host writes `sa_cache.db` exclusively, never `workbench.db`; future ingest clients each get their own isolated cache DB / inbox file; main app reads SA via `ATTACH DATABASE 'file:...?mode=ro'` (URI form mandatory; storage-isolation tests assert writes to `sa.*` fail with `SQLITE_READONLY`). Migration cut #1 (`ProfileLocator` + `app/001_research_reports.sql` + manifest + lock + cross-machine smoke) does NOT open yet — docs cleanup queued first per `docs/design/DOCS_GOVERNANCE_AUDIT_2026_05.md` to remove pre-pivot entrypoint pollution before code work begins.
- **2026-05-02 (formal product repositioning — local-first financial research agent workbench, Phase C paused)**: This is more than a deployment-target pivot. The project formally transitions from "agent dual-SDK refactor + service expansion (a backend with tools)" to **local-first financial research agent workbench (a product)**. **North star**: the agent reads its own accumulated knowledge substrate across sessions and machines; the user sees and edits everything via a research GUI; zipping the profile directory moves a researcher's work between machines. The product combines Hermes-like agent capabilities (persistent memory, skills, tools, scheduler, replay, compression, subagents) with this codebase's existing financial data layer (news / SA / macro / prices / fundamentals / signals) and a research GUI (ticker workspace, evidence browser, memory/notes, scheduled jobs, reports, source health, agent investigation trace). **Five-layer architecture**: (1) **Workbench UI** — research workspace (NOT admin dashboard; P1.5 minimal admin UI is repositioned to ops/health view only); (2) **Agent Layer** — Hermes-capability stack on existing dual-SDK paths (Phase C will eventually unify but is paused); (3) **Data Layer** — ingestion + caches + FTS5 + future vector + cross-source joins; (4) **Profile Layer** — single local profile directory (SQLite app DB + DuckDB/Parquet OLAP + raw files + reports + memories + skills + exports); (5) **Portability Layer** — zip-and-go + single-writer lock + manual export/import; v2 selective sync. **Hermes is capability reference, NOT product reference**: borrow filesystem-first profile + daemon scheduler (`~/.hermes/cron/jobs.json` + 60s gateway tick) + FTS5 cross-session recall + skills/tools flexibility; differentiation = financial data layer + investment research workflow, not general-purpose agent platform. **Phase C paused** — runner refactor is now second-order to product boundary / profile / sync / GUI IA / agent-to-data API. Spec at `docs/design/PHASE_C_UNIFIED_RUNNER_SPEC.md` preserved as-is (closures all valid). Resume gate (all three): workbench v1 ships + 2 weeks stable single-user use + ≥1 verified cross-machine migration. (ii) CLI-first workflow → GUI-first workflow (CLI becomes test/dev tool); (iii) Phase C runner refactor immediate priority → paused. **What survives unchanged (foundation, not throwaway)**: P0.1 replay net, P1.4 compression, job_runs persistence, SA ingestion + comment intelligence, macro calendar, tools registry, skills system, episodic memory tools, file attachment system, prompt caching. The agent capability stack is already ~80% of Hermes parity; the gap is filesystem-first profile + daemon scheduler + FTS5, not capability rewrites. legacy reads were transitional; dual-backend mode rejected (multiplies query / migration / test cost); (b) DuckDB added but strictly OLAP/columnar (news / price / macro / backtest / cross-source) — SQLite owns app state / job_runs / memory / settings / watchlist / notes / FTS5; DuckDB `ATTACH` SQLite for cross-engine joins; (c) packaging deferred — spec only locks deployment-target invariant (local web app or desktop wrapper must read same local profile directory); PyInstaller / launcher / Tauri choice waits for scheduler daemon model + storage profile to settle; (d) audit done before spec — without it spec is a wishlist. **Active sequence**: `docs/design/LOCAL_FIRST_RESEARCH_WORKBENCH_AUDIT.md` (file name explicitly carries the new positioning) → `LOCAL_FIRST_RESEARCH_WORKBENCH_SPEC.md` → read-only UI skeleton against existing reads → SQLite/DuckDB migration first cut (kill criterion: zip-and-go works on a second machine). **Six refinement locks** (not re-litigated): (a) sync surface is the main axis, not an appendix — "能不能跨機同步是根本問題"; (b) read-only prototype lists future write actions even if not implemented (else DTOs will be wrong-shape); (c) DTOs bidirectional from day one (`ReportDTO` + `ReportCreateInput` + `ReportUpdateInput`, three together); (d) "first migration cut" = read-only-prototype demand + zip-and-go smoke (NOT "migrate everything"); (e) Phase C resume gate is the three-AND above (NOT "when boundary feels stable"); (f) cross-machine smoke at mid-stream, not end — first SQLite table migrated triggers immediate second-machine test. **Risk**: SQLite has no GIN/tsvector — `agent_memories` (sql/004) and `news_search` (sql/006) need FTS5 redesign + tokenizer choice; Chinese support requires custom tokenizer / jieba pre-tokenize / trigram fallback. Spec must size that explicitly. **Repo identity implication**: `MindfulRL-Intraday` no longer matches the positioning. ArkScope rename Phase 2 (P3.2) becomes higher-priority once workbench v1 ships; README + `CLAUDE.md` content rewrite scheduled for spec phase (flag, not action). **Reference projects (capability or shape, not blueprints)**: Hermes Agent (filesystem + daemon pattern), FinceptTerminal (Qt6+C++ native — too heavy a stack but validates "single-binary research terminal" concept), `daily_stock_analysis` (Python + web workbench + scheduler — closer shape but batch-then-push, not interactive workbench).

(2) **`AgentEvent` imported only from `src/agents/shared/events.py`**; `runner/message_types.py` owns only `ToolCall` / `ToolResult` / `TurnResult`. - **2026-05-02 (Phase C spec follow-up)**: Spec review pass 1 closed three High findings + two Medium + one Low in `docs/design/PHASE_C_UNIFIED_RUNNER_SPEC.md`. (1) **OpenAI raw Responses API moved out of v1**: reviewer flagged that switching from `openai-agents` SDK to raw `openai.responses.create()` is a behaviour migration (event/history/retry/tool-loop semantics), not a refactor — replay net only partially covers SDK drift. v1 now keeps `OpenAIRunner` as an SDK-`Runner.run()` wrapper that transforms output to the unified shape; SDK stays installed through commit 7. Raw API migration relocated to a new §9 "Phase C v2" placeholder — separate spec, separate cycle, parked unless a concrete trigger emerges. (2) **Commit count drift**: prose said "7 commits", table had 0-7 = 8 entries. Clarified: 7 implementation commits (1-7) + spec landing as commit 0. (3) **Compaction asymmetry**: matrix row 11 falsely claimed both providers wire compaction via `ContextManager` — actually only Anthropic does; OpenAI uses `OpenAIResponsesCompactionSession` (SDK-driven server-side). Row split into 11a (Anthropic client-side via `ContextManager`/`ContextCompressor`) + 11b (OpenAI server-side via SDK) — Phase C preserves the asymmetry, does NOT try to unify. (4) Registry count 49 → "registered built-in tool table" (current count is 55, but spec shouldn't hard-code; tests own count). (5) "call existing builders unchanged" → "consume `shared/server_tools.py` directly" (the old `_build_*_tools_list` shims get deleted in commit 7, so the unified runner must not depend on them). - **2026-05-02 (spike done)**: P1.5 OSS spike closed. Decision doc at `docs/design/P1_5_S3_OSS_SPIKE_DECISION.md`. Superset rejected on deployment overhead + BI-shape mismatch (use case is ops monitoring, not data exploration); RQ Dashboard / Prefect UI rejected as out-of-scope (we have an in-process scheduler, no queue). Custom minimal passes the four non-negotiables: no ORM, single-process, reuses existing pydantic-typed JSON read paths (`/jobs/status`, `/jobs/history`, `/sa/market-news/health`, `/macro/health`, `/reports`), read-only default. v1 estimated 300-500 LoC under `src/api/admin/` (Jinja2 templates + thin route module). HTMX is a v1.1 additive choice, not v1. Integration is NOT next — P1.5 stops at the decision doc, Phase C spec begins immediately. When integration cycle opens, the decision doc is the input.
- **2026-05-02**: Next-step decision after P0.1 full-v1 + P1.4 land. Selected sequence: (1) P1.5 strict 1-day OSS spike — decision doc ONLY (sqladmin / FastAPI Admin / Superset / minimal-custom); explicit non-goals: no UI integration, no styling, no "while we're at it" features. (2) Phase C spec — no code yet, locks v1 boundaries + feature parity matrix vs current dual-SDK paths + replay-gate use during cutover + flag-rollout ramp + commit chain (will mirror P1.4's 5-commit shape). Phase C code opens only after spec review. Rationale: Phase C is the highest-leverage P2 item but has the highest blast radius — replay safety net is exactly what was built for it, but rushing into the refactor without a written spec wastes the safety net. P1.5 spike between is a bounded, interruptible context-switch that delivers a real artifact (decision doc) before re-entering the main thrust. Kept deferred: P0.4 anti-bot fallback / detail backfill / p95 monitoring (waiting for telemetry to indicate chronic problem); P2.2 Knowledge Graph (SA + News data not yet mature); P2.4 Key Markets (opportunistic only, must not preempt main thrust).

- **2026-04-30**: P1.4 Compression Phase B 5/5 landed in 5 commits + 5 review-fix passes (`99f90c9` spec → `05960fc` overflow_store → `15f4259` Layers 0-3 → `08bbbb0` ContextCompressor wiring → `eaf13c0` observability + /overflow CLI → `1ed4d77` Layer 5 + 6 → `95ff3cb` final follow-up). All five spec guarantees held end-to-end (library-not-runner, subagent path unchanged, Layer 0 retrieval-on-demand, no fabricated reasoning summaries, per-layer toggles). Default OFF maintained: master `compaction.enabled=true` ships the always-cheap layers, but `compaction_layer_5_enabled=false` keeps the LLM-call layer dormant until explicitly opted in or armed for one turn via `/compact`. Layer 4 (provider-native compaction) is wired as opt-in adapter; Layer 5 caller defaults to `claude-sonnet-4-6` streaming with 3-failure circuit breaker; Layer 6 anchor recovery is hard-capped at 1 KB. `get_overflow_payload` retrieval helper retained CLI-only — gated agent exposure deferred to v1.1 to avoid an unbounded prompt-injection vector. Three review-driven design changes made it through (none structural): (1) commit-4 `/overflow show` was bypassing the OverflowStore integrity gate — fixed in `b3d44e2`; (2) commit-5 `/compact` was silently no-op'ing under the planned default config because the CLI only built `AnthropicSummaryCaller` when `layer_5_enabled=True` — fixed in `95ff3cb`; (3) commit-5 `apply_layer_5` was conflating boundary-noop with caller-failure → three rapid `/compact` attempts on a short history would burn the circuit breaker with zero actual LLM faults — fixed by switching to `Layer5Outcome` literal (`success` / `noop` / `failed`) in `95ff3cb`. 308 tests across compressor + adjacent layers (≈230 net new for P1.4). Unblocks Phase C (Unified Runner) — replay capture wired into Anthropic path already covers compaction-triggered turns.
- **2026-04-28**: P1.4 Compression Phase B framing locked. Decision: compaction is a **steady-state operating layer**, not a defensive cap. Today's path with `server_compaction=false` means large tool results dump straight into the prompt; P1.4 turns that into "summary in-prompt + raw on disk (retrievable)". Borrowed three patterns from `~/PycharmProjects/AI_Agent_Researcher` (marker-based summary, recent boundary, circuit breaker) but rejected drop-in code (their `ClientCompactionSession` is OpenAI-Responses-only; we need dual-provider) and rejected OpenAI SDK Session injection (proven painful in their codebase). Provider-native compaction (Anthropic `compact-2026-01-12`, OpenAI `responses.compact()`) kept as Layer 4 *optional* adapters, not the default path. Five guarantees in the spec stop scope creep: (1) compressor is a library not a runner — future multi-round work reuses without rewriting, (2) subagent path unchanged, (3) Layer 0 turns "always-dump" into "retrieve-on-demand" — net positive for heavy-tool queries, (4) no fabricated reasoning summaries (drop or verbatim, never invent), (5) per-layer toggles + master `/compaction off`. `get_overflow_payload` retrieval helper kept CLI-only in v1 to avoid creating an unbridled prompt-injection vector; v1.1 candidate is gated agent exposure with size_cap + session_id binding + audit log. Multi-round runner explicitly deferred (Phase C / P2.x). Spec at `docs/design/P1_4_SPEC.md`; 5-commit plan locked.
- **2026-04-27**: P1.3 SA Digest / Reading Workflow v1 landed in 3 commits + 4 follow-ups (`ce5f780` spec → `1108310` 5 review findings → `55c02bd` tool+tests → `70065c5` 4 commit-1 follow-ups → `a19441e` registry+bridges → `68a60a5` dispatch regression+stale docstrings → `fd9fb80` envelope assertion → commit-3 skill prompts+acceptance tests+status flip). Locked-in shape: `get_sa_digest(ticker, days=14, max_articles=5, max_news=5, max_comments=8, min_comment_score=4.0)` returns deterministic evidence pack with `recent_articles[]`, `high_discussion_news[]`, `high_value_comments.{ticker_mentions[], candidate_mentions[]}`, `data_quality`, `source_notes[]`. Selection rules in SQL (layered CTE: per-article cap of 3 applied BEFORE per-kind `rn_per_kind` to avoid underfill); `needs_verification=true` rows pass through as flagged claims; `min_comment_score` clamped to `[0.0, 10.0]`; ticker uppercased reaching SQL params; `keyword_buckets` shape preserved as `Dict[str, List[str]]`. Skill prompt updates: `earnings_prep` mandates `days=30` call (added to `data_sources.required`), `full_analysis` recommends `days=14` (added to `data_sources.optional`). 22 unit tests + 2 skill body tests + 1 dispatch regression test + 12-file count-assertion sync. Bridge counts after wiring: registry 55, news category 8, Anthropic + OpenAI bridges 56 each. The reframe-then-narrow-then-test pattern proved valuable: review at every commit caught the SQL underfill case, the `keyword_buckets` shape drift, and the wrapper-removed-still-passes envelope assertion gap before they shipped.
- **2026-04-27 (revision)**: P1.3 reframed from "SA Latest Articles metadata v1" → "SA Digest / Reading Workflow v1". Diagnosis: existing SA data (Alpha Picks articles, Market News, Stage 1 comment signals) is already in DB but the agent doesn't surface it in decision flows; the bottleneck is **usage**, not coverage. v1 ships a deterministic evidence-pack tool `get_sa_digest(ticker, days=14, ...)` that composes the three existing tables with locked selection rules (≤3 comments per article, `min_comment_score=4.0` default, `needs_verification` rows kept, ticker-vs-candidate mention split preserved). **No internal LLM call** in the tool — main agent produces Chinese summary in its own context, keeping the test surface deterministic and avoiding nested LLM cost/retry/timeout/prompt-injection (gpt-5.5 review during spec). Skill prompt updates target `earnings_prep` (must call, days=30) and `full_analysis` (recommended, days=14); `portfolio_scan` deferred until SA-coverage rate is observed. Out of scope for v1: new SA data sources (transcripts / StockTalks / author tracking), server-side scrapers (kept out forever — extension is the only ingress), market-wide digest, internal LLM summary (v1.1 candidate: `summary_mode="llm"`). Spec at `docs/design/P1_3_SPEC.md`.
- **2026-04-27**: P1.2 Free calendar / macro layer landed in 6 commits + 2 follow-ups (`e8fc1db` schema/DAL → `f03cf86`/`6536652`/`959c589` FRED → `d3ec85a`/`5b376aa`/`35e8d4f` Finnhub + rename `src/p1_2`→`src/macro_calendar` → `2edea5e`/`3bde0ec`/`6cca46a` jobs+API → `b8454a2`/`a5b24d3` health → `2478e74` read API+tools → `7eeffb0`/`95a644d` review follow-ups). Locked-in shape: 6 jobs gated on `macro_calendar.enabled`, 4 read routes (`/macro/economic-calendar`, `/macro/earnings-calendar`, `/macro/ipo-calendar`, `/macro/series/{series_id}`), 2 agent tools (`get_economic_calendar`, `get_macro_value`), `/macro/health[?strict]`. `as_of` ISO contract: timestamps honoured at second precision, date-only inputs map to end-of-day UTC for `to_date`/`as_of` and start-of-day UTC for `from_date` (split via `_parse_iso_datetime_start` / `_parse_iso_datetime_end`). FRED ALFRED replay pinned to `output_type=1` (full revisions) / `=4` (initial release only) so future default changes can't silently shift semantics. Earnings calendar uses per-symbol Finnhub queries because `/calendar/earnings` ranges miss watchlist symbols on free tier. `importance` filter accepts CSV (e.g. `'high,medium'`) — earlier enum-only schema would have rejected the documented form.

Changed to `contribution = impact` so per-factor contributions sum to `composite_score` — the explainability contract. New invariant test asserts the additive property. `synthesize_signal`'s flat factors dict left as-is for backward compatibility (display weighting, not additive decomposition).

Real factor names (`SECTOR_MOMENTUM` / `EVENT_CHAIN` / `SENTIMENT_ANOMALY` / `VOLUME_SPIKE` / `EXTREME_SENTIMENT`) — explicitly NOT renamed to value/quality/momentum/low-vol since those factors don't exist here. `volume_spike` URL param maps to output factor_type `VOLUME_SPIKE`, not synthesizer input bucket `volume_anomaly`. Signal validation (forward-return decile analysis) deferred to a future "signal validation" cycle; entry/exit logic deferred to a strategy/backtest layer. SA comments deliberately not wired in — Stage 1 has no direction.

## 11. Open Questions

1. **Replay Harness scope**: Capture token-level streaming or only final response? (Token-level enables post-hoc partial-output analysis but doubles capture size.)
2. **S2 schema separation**: Single `job_runs` table vs `job_definitions` + `job_runs`? (Separation enables config-driven job catalog; merge enables faster build.)
3. **S3 OSS candidates to spike**: Top 3? My current pick is FastAPI Admin + sqladmin + RQ Dashboard, but Prefect UI has stronger job-history features. Need user decision before spike.
4. **SA comment rule-set versioning**: Inline `rule_set_version` string + git-tracked rule file, or DB-stored rule definitions for hot-reload?
5. **FRED API key**: ✅ resolved 2026-04-26. Free key is required (no usable anonymous quota); registered and in `config/.env`. See `P1_2_PROVIDER_DISCOVERY.md` §6 for smoke results.
6. **Subscription evidence threshold**: How many days of OOS validation before approving a research subscription? (Suggestion: 30 trading days minimum, with predefined success metric.)
